  ---
  P6.1 Plan: Gate A Test (tools/verify_methodology.py)

  Deliverable: tools/verify_methodology.py — CLI tool that verifies 36 methodology docs exist.

  Gate A Criterion: 3 steps × 4 document types × 3 versions = 36 methodology documents stored in the artifacts table with artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'.

  Files to Create/Modify

  | File                        | Action |
  |-----------------------------|--------|
  | tools/verify_methodology.py | Create |

  Approach (5 bullets)

  1. CLI interface: Accept --steps argument (e.g., --steps 1 2 3), optional --workflow-id for specific workflow verification, and --database-url override (default from env/config).
  2. Query artifacts table: Count methodology docs matching:
    - pass_type = 'definition'
    - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
    - step_number IN (1, 2, 3)
    - Group by (step_name, document_type, document_version)
  3. Validation logic:
    - Expected: 36 rows (3 steps × 4 doc types × 3 versions)
    - Per-step breakdown: 12 docs each (4 types × 3 versions)
    - Document types: data_sheet, todo_list, guidance, detailed_procedure
    - Versions: v1, v2, v3
  4. Output format:
    - Print summary table showing doc counts
    - Exit 0 if count = 36, exit 1 otherwise
    - --verbose flag for detailed per-document listing
  5. No network/LLM dependencies: Pure database query, uses existing async DB infrastructure from apps/api/infrastructure/postgres.py.

  Questions (if blocked)

  None — the spec is clear on Gate A requirements.

  ---
  Awaiting plan approval to implement P6.1.

<Co-Developer-1>
Findings

  - --workflow-id as UUID targets artifacts.workflow_id (internal PK) but API/
    public ID is workflows.workflow_id (string); this will be confusing and
    likely wrong in real use. See apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/workflow.py.
  - Defaulting to “All workflows” can overcount and misreport Gate A; the gate
    is per‑workflow and expects exactly 36 docs for a single workflow. See Gate
    A in docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md.
  - Adding count_methodology_docs_grouped in apps/api/infrastructure/db/
    repositories/artifact.py expands core code during verification; not required
    for P6.1 and adds surface area late in the cycle.
  - Counting alone is insufficient for “valid content” in Appendix A; you should
    also ensure content_markdown is present/non‑empty and filter
    artifact_type='methodology_doc' and pass_type='definition'. See apps/api/
    infrastructure/db/models/artifact.py.

  What’s good

  - Scope stays within P6.1 and aligns with the 3×4×3 methodology structure; doc
    types/versions are correct.
  - Output/reporting and exit codes are sensible for a CLI gate check.

  Best path to meet the objective

  - Keep P6.1 self‑contained: implement tools/verify_methodology.py without
    modifying apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py by using
    ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow plus in‑script grouping.
  - Accept external workflow_id (string), resolve it to internal UUID via
    WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id; if not provided, select the most
    recent workflow or fail when ambiguous.
  - Enforce filters and uniqueness per step/type/version and validate non‑empty
    content_markdown to satisfy Appendix A.

Risks / Gaps

  - Gate A tooling referenced in docs is missing: tools/ is empty
    (only .gitkeep), so tools/verify_methodology.py doesn’t exist even though
    it’s required in docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md and README.md.
  - Make targets for gates are missing: Makefile doesn’t define test-gates /
    test-recovery / e2e, even though they’re referenced in specs and README.md.
  - Gate E coverage appears absent: no e2e tests under apps/api/tests/ or tests/
    e2e/.
  - Gate B trace tooling is explicitly deferred per docs/DECISIONS.md; avoid
    re‑introducing /workflows/{id}/traceability or tools/verify_traces.py
    without approval.

</Co-Developer-1> 

⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1 Feedback

  Co-Developer-1 raises four valid concerns. Here's my analysis:

  1. --workflow-id UUID vs String — Agree, must fix

  | My Plan                                                   | Co-Developer-1                                                   | Verdict                   |
  |-----------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------|
  | Accept UUID targeting artifacts.workflow_id (internal PK) | Accept string workflows.workflow_id (public ID), resolve to UUID | Co-Developer-1 is correct |

  The public API uses string workflow IDs like "wf-abc123". Users will copy these from API responses. My plan asks for the internal UUID which users won't have. Fix: accept string, resolve via WorkflowRepository.

  2. "All Workflows" Default — Agree, must fix

  | My Plan                           | Co-Developer-1                                    | Verdict                   |
  |-----------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|---------------------------|
  | Default to scanning all workflows | Gate A is per-workflow (36 docs for ONE workflow) | Co-Developer-1 is correct |

  Aggregating across workflows is misleading. A workflow with 18 docs + another with 18 docs = 36 total but neither passes Gate A. Fix: require --workflow-id OR auto-select most recent workflow (with warning).

  3. Adding Method to ArtifactRepository — Agree, prefer self-contained

  | My Plan                                                 | Co-Developer-1                                        | Verdict                                |
  |---------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|
  | Add count_methodology_docs_grouped() to production code | Use existing list_for_workflow() + in-script grouping | Co-Developer-1 has the better approach |

  Rationale:
  - P6 is verification phase, not feature development
  - Adding methods to production code increases surface area and test burden
  - A verification tool should be self-contained and use existing APIs
  - Python grouping of ~36 artifacts is trivial performance-wise

  4. Content Validation Beyond Counting — Agree, must add

  | My Plan         | Co-Developer-1                         | Verdict                   |
  |-----------------|----------------------------------------|---------------------------|
  | Count rows only | Validate content_markdown is non-empty | Co-Developer-1 is correct |

  Gate A says "methodology exists" — an empty doc doesn't satisfy this. Must also enforce:
  - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
  - pass_type = 'definition'
  - content_markdown IS NOT NULL AND content_markdown != ''

  ---
  Revised Approach

  CHANGES FROM ORIGINAL PLAN:

  1. NO modifications to apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py
     - Use ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow() and filter/group in Python

  2. Accept --workflow-id as STRING (external ID)
     - Resolve to internal UUID via WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id()
     - If not provided: auto-select most recent workflow with warning

  3. Validate content, not just existence
     - Filter: artifact_type='methodology_doc', pass_type='definition'
     - Check: content_markdown is non-empty
     - Report: "Step 1 / data_sheet / v2: MISSING" or "EMPTY"

  4. Single workflow scope
     - Gate A = 36 docs for ONE workflow, not aggregated

  ---
  My Recommendation

  Adopt Co-Developer-1's approach with one clarification:

  The suggestion to use WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id() assumes that method exists. I should verify it does, or use an equivalent query. The existing WorkflowRepository likely has get_by_id(uuid) but may need the string-based lookup.

  Files to create (revised):

  | File                        | Action                  |
  |-----------------------------|-------------------------|
  | tools/verify_methodology.py | Create (self-contained) |

  No modifications to production code in apps/api/.

  Should I update the plan file with these revisions?

<Co-Developer-1>
What’s correct

  - Gate A target is clear: 3 steps × 4 doc types × 3 versions = 36 docs.
  - Producing a CLI tool under tools/ is the right scope for P6.1.

  Must fix

  - Workflow ID handling: Tool must accept the public workflow_id string (e.g.,
    wf-...) and resolve to internal UUID via
    WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id(). Do not ask users for internal
    UUIDs.
  - Per‑workflow scope: Gate A is per workflow. Do not aggregate across all
    workflows. Either require --workflow-id or support --latest explicitly.
  - Content validation: Counting rows alone is insufficient. Validate
    content_markdown is present and non‑empty. Empty docs must fail.
  - Filtering: Ensure query filters to artifact_type='methodology_doc' and
    pass_type='definition' and steps 1–3 only.

  Should avoid

  - Modifying production repositories for verification tooling (keep P6.1
    self‑contained).
  - Fragile import hacks; mirror existing conventions by adding apps/api to
    sys.path and importing config / infrastructure.* directly.

  Recommended shape

  - One file only: tools/verify_methodology.py.
  - Inputs: --workflow-id (string, required) and optional --steps (default 1 2
    3), optional --database-url.
  - Behavior: Resolve workflow UUID, query artifacts, group by step_number,
    document_type, document_version, check non‑empty content_markdown, and
    enforce exactly one per combination.
  - Exit codes: 0 pass, 1 fail, 2 DB error, 3 workflow not found.
  - Output: Grid with MISSING/EMPTY statuses + summary.
</Co-Developer-1> 

⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1 Feedback (Round 2)

  This feedback refines and confirms the previous review. Let me assess each element:

  Confirmed Decisions

  | Item                       | Status    |
  |----------------------------|-----------|
  | 36 docs target (3×4×3)     | ✅ Agreed |
  | CLI tool in tools/         | ✅ Agreed |
  | No production code changes | ✅ Agreed |

  Key Refinement: --workflow-id Required

  | Previous Revision                | Co-Developer-1 (Round 2) | My Assessment       |
  |----------------------------------|--------------------------|---------------------|
  | Optional, with --latest fallback | Required (string)        | Agree with required |

  Rationale for requiring --workflow-id:
  - Explicit > implicit — user knows exactly what they're verifying
  - Avoids ambiguity about "latest" semantics (by created_at? updated_at?)
  - Simpler implementation, fewer edge cases
  - If --latest is needed, it can be a future enhancement

  Content Validation Emphasis

  Co-Developer-1 is explicit: "Empty docs must fail."

  This means the tool must check:
  # Not just existence, but valid content
  if artifact.content_markdown is None or artifact.content_markdown.strip() == "":
      status = "EMPTY"  # Fails Gate A

  Uniqueness Constraint

  New detail: "enforce exactly one per combination"

  This catches a subtle bug — if somehow two v1 data_sheets exist for Step 1, that's anomalous. The tool should:
  - Expect exactly 1 artifact per (step, doc_type, version) tuple
  - Flag duplicates as warnings or errors

  ---
  Final Perspective

  I fully agree with Co-Developer-1's recommended shape. It is:

  1. Minimal — single file, no production code changes
  2. Explicit — requires workflow-id, no magic defaults
  3. Thorough — validates content, not just counts
  4. Clear — defined exit codes and output format

  Implementation Summary

  tools/verify_methodology.py

  INPUTS:
    --workflow-id   STRING (required) - public workflow ID
    --steps         INT... (optional) - default: 1 2 3
    --database-url  STRING (optional) - override from settings

  BEHAVIOR:
    1. Resolve workflow_id string → internal UUID
    2. Query artifacts WHERE:
       - workflow_id = <resolved UUID>
       - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
       - pass_type = 'definition'
       - step_number IN (1, 2, 3)
    3. Group by (step_number, document_type, document_version)
    4. For each of 36 expected combinations:
       - MISSING: no artifact found
       - EMPTY: artifact exists but content_markdown is null/blank
       - OK: artifact exists with content
       - DUPLICATE: more than one artifact (warning)
    5. Exit 0 if all 36 are OK, else exit 1

  EXIT CODES:
    0 - PASS (36 valid docs)
    1 - FAIL (missing/empty docs)
    2 - ERROR (database connection)
    3 - ERROR (workflow not found)

  OUTPUT:
    Grid showing status per step/type/version + summary

  ---
  Ready to update the plan file with this final specification and proceed to implementation.

     P6.1 Plan: Gate A Verification Tool (Final)

     Objective

     Create tools/verify_methodology.py - CLI tool to verify 36 methodology docs exist with valid 
     content (Gate A criterion).

     Gate A Criterion

     - 3 steps × 4 document types × 3 versions = 36 methodology documents
     - Document types: data_sheet, todo_list, guidance, detailed_procedure
     - Document versions: v1, v2, v3
     - Steps (MVP): 1, 2, 3
     - Content must be non-empty (not just existence)

     Files to Create

     | File                        | Action | Purpose                 |
     |-----------------------------|--------|-------------------------|
     | tools/verify_methodology.py | Create | Self-contained CLI tool |

     No modifications to production code in apps/api/.

     CLI Specification

     Arguments

     | Argument       | Type   | Required | Default       | Description                          |
     |----------------|--------|----------|---------------|--------------------------------------|
     | --workflow-id  | STRING | Yes      | —             | Public workflow ID (e.g., wf-abc123) |
     | --steps        | INT... | No       | 1 2 3         | Steps to verify                      |
     | --database-url | STRING | No       | From settings | Override DB connection               |
     | --verbose / -v | Flag   | No       | False         | Show detailed output                 |

     Exit Codes

     | Code | Meaning                                     |
     |------|---------------------------------------------|
     | 0    | PASS - All 36 docs found with valid content |
     | 1    | FAIL - Missing or empty docs                |
     | 2    | ERROR - Database connection failed          |
     | 3    | ERROR - Workflow not found                  |

     Behavior

     1. Resolve workflow ID: Accept public workflow_id string → resolve to internal UUID via 
     WorkflowRepository
     2. Query artifacts with filters:
       - workflow_id = <resolved UUID>
       - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
       - pass_type = 'definition'
       - step_number IN (1, 2, 3)
     3. Group by (step_number, document_type, document_version)
     4. Validate each of 36 combinations:
       - MISSING - No artifact found
       - EMPTY - Artifact exists but content_markdown is null/blank
       - OK - Artifact exists with valid content
       - DUPLICATE - More than one artifact (warning)
     5. Exit 0 only if all 36 are OK

     Output Format

     Gate A Verification Report
     ==========================
     Workflow: wf-abc123
     Steps: 1, 2, 3

     Step 1: problem_definition
     --------------------------
     Document Type        v1       v2       v3
     data_sheet           OK       OK       OK
     todo_list            OK       OK       OK
     guidance             OK       MISSING  OK
     detailed_procedure   OK       OK       EMPTY

     Step 2: requirements
     --------------------------
     ...

     Summary
     =======
     Expected: 36
     OK:       34
     Missing:  1
     Empty:    1
     Status:   FAIL

     Implementation Approach

     Self-Contained Design

     - Use existing ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow()
     - Filter and group in Python (no new repository methods)
     - Import via sys.path pointing to apps/api

     Key Query Logic

     # Fetch all artifacts for workflow
     artifacts = await artifact_repo.list_for_workflow(workflow_uuid)

     # Filter to methodology docs
     methodology_docs = [
         a for a in artifacts
         if a.artifact_type == "methodology_doc"
         and a.pass_type == PassType.DEFINITION
         and a.step_number in steps_to_check
     ]

     # Group by (step, type, version) and validate content
     for step in steps:
         for doc_type in DocumentType:
             for version in DocumentVersion:
                 matches = [a for a in methodology_docs if ...]
                 if len(matches) == 0:
                     status = "MISSING"
                 elif not matches[0].content_markdown or not matches[0].content_markdown.strip():
                     status = "EMPTY"
                 else:
                     status = "OK"

     Verification Command

     python tools/verify_methodology.py --workflow-id wf-abc123
     # Exit 0 if all 36 docs exist with content

     Dependencies

     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py (existing, read-only)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow.py (existing, read-only)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/enums.py (DocumentType, DocumentVersion, PassType)
     - apps/api/config.py (settings.postgres_url)

  P6.2 Plan Summary

  Objective: Create tools/validate_schemas.py - a CLI tool to validate Pass 2 step packages against their JSON schemas.

  Key Points:

  - Required argument: --workflow-id (public workflow ID string)
  - Optional: --steps (default 1 2 3), --verbose, --database-url
  - Exit codes: 0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=DB error, 3=workflow not found

  Implementation:
  - Self-contained tool in tools/ (no production code changes)
  - Uses existing ArtifactRepository.get_step_package() for queries
  - Uses existing application/schemas.get_schema_for_step() for schema loading
  - Uses jsonschema.Draft7Validator for validation

  Scope Clarification:
  - Per docs/DECISIONS.md, trace validation (tools/verify_traces.py) is deferred
  - This slice covers ONLY schema validation, not traceability link verification

<Co-Developer-1>
P6.2 Plan (Gate B — Schema Validation Only)
  Files to create/modify

  - tools/validate_schemas.py (create)

  Approach summary (3–5 bullets)

  - Build a self‑contained CLI mirroring tools/verify_methodology.py with
    --workflow-id (required), --steps default 1 2 3, --database-url, --verbose;
    validate steps are 1–3 and dedupe/sort them.
  - Resolve the public workflow ID via WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id();
    fetch artifacts via ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow() and filter to
    artifact_type == "step_package" and pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION, then
    select the latest revision per step_number.
  - Map step_number -> domain.state.StepName for schema lookup
    (application.schemas.get_schema_for_step), validate content_jsonb with
    jsonschema.Draft7Validator, and emit per‑step status: VALID, INVALID,
    MISSING, EMPTY (optionally flag package_type mismatch).
  - Print a concise report + summary counts, return exit codes 0/1/2/3; keep
    Gate B scope limited to schema validation (trace checks deferred per docs/
    DECISIONS.md).
  - No apps/api edits; no new repo methods; no traceability tooling.

  Negative patterns to avoid

  - Don’t mix domain.state.StepName and infrastructure.db.models.StepName
    without an explicit mapping; use step_number mapping to the domain enum for
    schema lookup.
  - Don’t skip pass_type == execution and artifact_type == "step_package"
    filters; get_step_package() alone isn’t sufficient.
  - Don’t implement /workflows/{id}/traceability or tools/verify_traces.py
    (explicitly deferred).
  - Don’t default to “all workflows” or implicit “latest”; require explicit
    workflow targeting unless approved.
  - Don’t bypass application.schemas.get_schema_for_step with hard‑coded schema
    paths.

  Questions (if blocked)

  1. Should --workflow-id remain required, or add an explicit --latest flag and
     update docs accordingly?
  2. Should a package_type mismatch be treated as FAIL or a warning?
  3. Validate only the latest revision per step (default) or all revisions?
</Co-Developer-1> 

⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1 Feedback

  Co-Developer-1 raises several excellent points. Let me analyze each:

  1. Query Strategy: get_step_package() vs list_for_workflow() + filtering

  Co-Developer-1's approach:
  artifacts = await artifact_repo.list_for_workflow(workflow_uuid)
  step_packages = [a for a in artifacts
      if a.artifact_type == "step_package"
      and a.pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION]

  My plan's approach:
  artifact = await artifact_repo.get_step_package(workflow_id, step_name)

  My perspective: For a verification tool, Co-Developer-1's explicit filtering approach is superior. Verification tools should be transparent about what they're checking - relying on repository method internals is less auditable. I'll adopt this approach.

  2. StepName Enum Confusion

  Co-Developer-1 warns: "Don't mix domain.state.StepName and infrastructure.db.models.StepName"

  My perspective: This is a critical catch! Looking at the code:
  - application/schemas.py imports from domain.state import StepName
  - infrastructure/db/models/ has its own StepName enum
  - These may be the same enum or may differ

  Resolution: Use explicit step_number → domain.state.StepName mapping for schema lookup:
  from domain.state import StepName as DomainStepName

  STEP_NUMBER_TO_DOMAIN_STEP = {
      1: DomainStepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION,
      2: DomainStepName.REQUIREMENTS,
      3: DomainStepName.OBJECTIVES,
  }

  3. Status Values: EMPTY

  Co-Developer-1 adds: EMPTY status for content_jsonb = None

  My perspective: Agreed. A package could exist with null content - this is distinct from MISSING (no artifact) and INVALID (failed validation). Adding EMPTY maintains parity with verify_methodology.py.

  4. Package Type Mismatch

  Co-Developer-1 suggests: "optionally flag package_type mismatch"

  Question: Should mismatch be FAIL or warning?

  My perspective: Treat as FAIL (INVALID). If step 2 has package_type = "ProblemDefinitionPackage" instead of "RequirementsPackage", that's a structural error indicating data corruption or bug - not a valid state.

  5. Co-Developer-1's Questions

  | Question                                 | My Recommendation                                                                                                                        |
  |------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
  | --workflow-id required vs --latest flag? | Keep required. Verification tools should target explicit workflows. A --latest flag adds complexity without clear benefit for Gate B.    |
  | Package type mismatch: FAIL or warning?  | FAIL (INVALID). Structural integrity matters.                                                                                            |
  | Latest revision only or all revisions?   | Latest only (default). Gate B asks "does valid package exist?" not "are all revisions valid?" Could add --all-revisions later if needed. |

  ---
  Updated Plan Recommendations

  I'll update the plan to incorporate these refinements:

  1. Use explicit filtering (list_for_workflow() + filter) instead of get_step_package()
  2. Use domain.state.StepName explicitly for schema lookup with step_number mapping
  3. Add EMPTY status for null content_jsonb
  4. Validate package_type matches expected value (mismatch = INVALID)
  5. Keep --workflow-id required
  6. Validate latest revision only (per step)

     P6.2 Plan: Gate B Schema Validation Tool (Revised)

     Objective

     Create tools/validate_schemas.py - CLI tool to validate Pass 2 step packages against JSON schemas 
     (Gate B criterion: schema validation).

     Note: Per docs/DECISIONS.md, trace validation (tools/verify_traces.py and 
     /workflows/{id}/traceability endpoint) is deferred. This slice focuses ONLY on schema validation.

     Gate B Criterion (Schema Portion)

     - Step 1-3 Pass 2 packages exist with valid schemas
     - Each package (ProblemDefinitionPackage, RequirementsPackage, ObjectivesPackage) validates against
      its JSON schema
     - Packages stored in content_jsonb field with artifact_type="step_package", pass_type="execution"

     File to Create

     | File                      | Action | Purpose                                       |
     |---------------------------|--------|-----------------------------------------------|
     | tools/validate_schemas.py | Create | Self-contained CLI tool for schema validation |

     No modifications to production code in apps/api/.

     CLI Specification

     Arguments

     | Argument       | Type   | Required | Default       | Description                          |
     |----------------|--------|----------|---------------|--------------------------------------|
     | --workflow-id  | STRING | Yes      | —             | Public workflow ID (e.g., wf-abc123) |
     | --steps        | INT... | No       | 1 2 3         | Steps to validate                    |
     | --database-url | STRING | No       | From settings | Override DB connection               |
     | --verbose / -v | Flag   | No       | False         | Show detailed validation errors      |

     Exit Codes

     | Code | Meaning                                                  |
     |------|----------------------------------------------------------|
     | 0    | PASS - All expected step packages found and schema-valid |
     | 1    | FAIL - Missing, empty, or invalid packages               |
     | 2    | ERROR - Database connection failed                       |
     | 3    | ERROR - Workflow not found                               |

     Behavior

     1. Resolve workflow ID: Accept public workflow_id string → resolve to internal UUID via 
     WorkflowRepository
     2. Query step packages with explicit filtering:
       - Use ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow(workflow_id)
       - Filter explicitly: artifact_type == "step_package" AND pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION
       - Group by step_number, select latest revision per step
     3. Validate each package against JSON schema:
       - Map step_number → domain.state.StepName (NOT infrastructure.db.models.StepName)
       - Load schema via application/schemas.get_schema_for_step(domain_step_name)
       - Validate package_type matches expected value (mismatch = INVALID)
       - Use jsonschema.Draft7Validator to validate content_jsonb
     4. Report status per package:
       - VALID - Package exists, correct type, passes schema validation
       - INVALID - Package exists but fails validation or wrong package_type
       - EMPTY - Package exists but content_jsonb is None
       - MISSING - No package found for step
     5. Exit 0 only if all requested steps have VALID packages

     Output Format

     Gate B Schema Validation Report
     ===============================
     Workflow: wf-abc123
     Steps: 1, 2, 3

     Step 1: problem_definition
     --------------------------
     Package: ProblemDefinitionPackage
     Revision: 1
     Status: VALID

     Step 2: requirements
     --------------------------
     Package: RequirementsPackage
     Revision: 2
     Status: INVALID
     Errors:
       - path: ['requirements', 0, 'id']
         message: 'INVALID-ID' does not match '^(FR|NFR|CR|IR)-\\d{3}$'

     Step 3: objectives
     --------------------------
     Status: MISSING

     Summary
     =======
     Expected: 3
     Valid:    1
     Invalid:  1
     Missing:  1
     Status:   FAIL

     Implementation Approach

     Self-Contained Design (Following verify_methodology.py Pattern)

     #!/usr/bin/env python3
     import sys, os
     sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "apps", "api"))

     # Use domain.state.StepName for schema lookup (NOT infrastructure.db.models.StepName)
     from domain.state import StepName as DomainStepName
     from infrastructure.db.models import PassType
     from infrastructure.db.repositories import ArtifactRepository, WorkflowRepository
     from application.schemas import get_schema_for_step
     import jsonschema

     Step Number Mappings (Critical: Use domain.state.StepName)

     # Map step_number → domain.state.StepName for schema lookup
     STEP_NUMBER_TO_DOMAIN_STEP: Dict[int, DomainStepName] = {
         1: DomainStepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION,
         2: DomainStepName.REQUIREMENTS,
         3: DomainStepName.OBJECTIVES,
     }

     # Expected package_type per step (for structural validation)
     EXPECTED_PACKAGE_TYPE: Dict[int, str] = {
         1: "ProblemDefinitionPackage",
         2: "RequirementsPackage",
         3: "ObjectivesPackage",
     }

     Key Query Logic (Explicit Filtering)

     # Fetch all artifacts and filter explicitly (transparent for verification tool)
     all_artifacts = await artifact_repo.list_for_workflow(workflow_uuid)

     # Filter to Pass 2 step packages only
     step_packages = [
         a for a in all_artifacts
         if a.artifact_type == "step_package"
         and a.pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION
         and a.step_number in steps
     ]

     # Group by step_number, get latest revision per step
     for step_num in steps:
         matches = [a for a in step_packages if a.step_number == step_num]

         if not matches:
             status = "MISSING"
         else:
             # Get latest revision
             artifact = max(matches, key=lambda a: a.revision)

             if artifact.content_jsonb is None:
                 status = "EMPTY"
             elif artifact.package_type != EXPECTED_PACKAGE_TYPE[step_num]:
                 status = "INVALID"  # Wrong package type
                 errors = [{"message": f"Expected {EXPECTED_PACKAGE_TYPE[step_num]}, got 
     {artifact.package_type}"}]
             else:
                 # Validate against schema using domain.state.StepName
                 domain_step = STEP_NUMBER_TO_DOMAIN_STEP[step_num]
                 schema = get_schema_for_step(domain_step)
                 validator = jsonschema.Draft7Validator(schema)
                 errors = list(validator.iter_errors(artifact.content_jsonb))
                 status = "VALID" if not errors else "INVALID"

     Step-to-Schema Mapping

     | Step | StepName Enum      | Package Type             | Schema File                    |
     |------|--------------------|--------------------------|--------------------------------|
     | 1    | PROBLEM_DEFINITION | ProblemDefinitionPackage | problem_definition.schema.json |
     | 2    | REQUIREMENTS       | RequirementsPackage      | requirements.schema.json       |
     | 3    | OBJECTIVES         | ObjectivesPackage        | objectives.schema.json         |

     Verification Commands

     # Help
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --help

     # Validate all steps for workflow
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123

     # Verbose output with errors
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123 --verbose

     # Specific steps only
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123 --steps 1 2

     Dependencies (Read-Only)

     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py - get_step_package()
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow.py - get_by_workflow_id()
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/ - StepName, PassType
     - apps/api/application/schemas.py - get_schema_for_step()
     - packages/contracts/*.schema.json - Step 1-3 JSON schemas
     - apps/api/config.py - settings.postgres_url

     Not In Scope (Deferred per docs/DECISIONS.md)

     - tools/verify_traces.py - Traceability link verification
     - /workflows/{id}/traceability endpoint
     - Trace link validation (coverage_map, trace_map checks)

     Negative Patterns to Avoid (per Co-Developer-1)

     1. Don't mix StepName enums: Use domain.state.StepName for schema lookup, NOT 
     infrastructure.db.models.StepName
     2. Don't skip explicit filtering: Use list_for_workflow() + explicit artifact_type/pass_type 
     filters for transparency
     3. Don't implement deferred features: No traceability endpoint or verify_traces.py
     4. Don't default to "all workflows": Require explicit --workflow-id
     5. Don't bypass get_schema_for_step(): Use the application layer's schema loader, not hard-coded 
     paths

     Design Decisions

     | Decision                        | Rationale                                                      
             |
     |---------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------
     --------|
     | --workflow-id required          | Verification tools should target explicit workflows            
             |
     | Package type mismatch = INVALID | Structural integrity matters; wrong type indicates data 
     corruption     |
     | Latest revision only            | Gate B asks "does valid package exist?" not "are all revisions 
     valid?" |
     | Explicit filtering              | Verification tool should be transparent about what it checks   
             |
     | EMPTY status added              | Distinct from MISSING (no artifact) and INVALID (failed 
     validation)    |

     Test Scenarios

     1. --help shows usage
     2. Non-existent workflow returns exit code 3
     3. Workflow with valid packages returns exit code 0
     4. Workflow with missing packages returns exit code 1
     5. Workflow with invalid packages returns exit code 1 + errors in verbose mode
     6. Workflow with empty content_jsonb returns exit code 1 (EMPTY status)
     7. Workflow with wrong package_type returns exit code 1 (INVALID status)





⏺ Plan approved. Let me start implementation by setting up the task tracking and then creating the tool.

     │         config,                                                                                     │

     │     result2 = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)                                                     │
     │                                                                                                     │
     │     # Should interrupt again at same step (re-execution complete)                                   │
     │     assert_interrupted(result2)                                                                     │
     │     assert result2["current_step"] == original_step  # Same step                                    │
     │     assert result2["step_state"].status == StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW                               │

> evaluate the approach of Co-Developer-1.  Scrutinize the decisions made and compare with your 
directives above.  Reflect on the decisions made and give your perspective on what you think is best to 
achieve the objective.
<Co-Developer-1>
Plan focuses on deterministic Gate C tests and a dedicated make test-gates
  target, avoiding LLM/API network calls and keeping scope inside P6.3.

  Files

  - apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py (create)
  - Makefile (modify: add test-gates target, add to help, add to .PHONY)

  Approach (3–5 bullets)

  - Drive the LangGraph directly with SolverCheckpointSaver and a Pass 2
    WorkflowState to test gate behavior without API endpoints (Gate E covers
    API).
  - Add a monkeypatch fixture that stubs
    orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output and
    orchestration.nodes.validate_output to return deterministic output and
    ValidationResult(passed=True) so tests are offline and non‑flaky.
  - Implement Gate C scenarios:
      - C1: message does not advance (current_step unchanged, step_state.status
        == awaiting_review).
      - C2: resuming without approve does not advance (no step change; status
        stays awaiting_review).
      - C3: reject/revise loops back to awaiting_review on the same step.
  - Assert via graph.aget_state snapshots to confirm persisted state and prevent
    false positives.

  Verification

  - make test-gates (requires DB running; use make dev-db if needed)

  Questions (if needed)

  1. Should Gate C tests stay graph‑level (recommended), or must they hit API
     endpoints despite Gate E covering that flow?
  2. Is creating apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py acceptable, or do you
     prefer extending apps/api/tests/integration/
     test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py?

  Negative patterns to avoid

  - Allowing Gate C tests to call the real LLM adapter or require API keys/
    network access.
  - Using langgraph.checkpoint.postgres.PostgresSaver instead of
    SolverCheckpointSaver.
  - Broad test-gates targets that run Gate D/E or the full integration suite.
  - Assertions that allow message or “no decision” to advance current_step.
</Co-Developer-1> 

⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1's Approach

  Key Differences

  | Aspect         | My Plan                                                | Co-Developer-1                                   |
  |----------------|--------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|
  | File strategy  | Extend existing test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py | Create new test_gate_c.py                        |
  | LLM handling   | Not addressed                                          | Monkeypatch generate_step_output/validate_output |
  | C2 coverage    | Implied by C1 + approve test                           | Explicit "resume without approve" test           |
  | Scenario count | 3 (C1, C2 implied, C3)                                 | 3 explicit tests                                 |

  Critical Analysis

  1. Monkeypatching LLM Calls - Co-Developer-1 is correct

  I missed this. The directive explicitly states:
  "Prefer deterministic tests; avoid external LLM calls or network access where possible."

  The existing tests in test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py run graph.ainvoke() which executes structure_node → generate_step_output() (LLM call). Without stubbing, these tests either:
  - Require actual LLM access (violates directive)
  - Are flaky due to LLM variability
  - May pass accidentally due to error handling

  Verdict: Co-Developer-1's monkeypatch approach is essential for deterministic Gate C tests.

  2. New File vs. Extending Existing

  Co-Developer-1 proposes test_gate_c.py. Arguments:

  | For new file                         | For extending existing       |
  |--------------------------------------|------------------------------|
  | Clean separation of Gate C tests     | Avoids fixture duplication   |
  | Self-contained with monkeypatching   | Tests already exist and work |
  | Clearer make test-gates target       | Less code to maintain        |
  | Doesn't risk breaking existing tests | Consistent testing approach  |

  My perspective: A dedicated test_gate_c.py is cleaner IF it includes proper monkeypatching. The existing tests can remain as "smoke tests" that run with LLM (for P6.5 E2E perhaps), while the new file provides deterministic Gate C verification.

  3. Explicit C2 Test

  My plan treated C2 ("cannot advance without approve") as implied by C1 (message doesn't advance) + the approve test. Co-Developer-1 adds an explicit test for "resuming without approve does not advance."

  Verdict: Explicit is better. The spec says "attempt to advance without calling /actions/approve" should fail. A direct test that tries to resume with no decision (or MESSAGE) and verifies no advancement is clearer.

  4. Graph-Level Focus

  Co-Developer-1 correctly identifies that Gate C tests should stay at graph level. Gate E (P6.5) covers API endpoints. This is the right separation:
  - Gate C: Proves the state machine prevents unauthorized advancement
  - Gate E: Proves the API correctly interfaces with that state machine

  Verdict: Agree with graph-level testing for Gate C.

  Answers to Co-Developer-1's Questions

  Q1: Should Gate C tests stay graph-level or hit API endpoints?

  Graph-level is correct. Gate C is about the state machine logic, not API behavior. The criterion "cannot advance without approve" is enforced by route_after_decision() in the graph, not by the API endpoints. Gate E should validate the API surface.

  Q2: New file or extend existing?

  I now prefer the new test_gate_c.py because:
  1. It allows clean monkeypatching without affecting existing tests
  2. make test-gates can target it precisely
  3. The existing tests serve a different purpose (full integration with LLM)

  Recommended Approach

  Accept Co-Developer-1's approach with these specifics:

  1. Create apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py with:
    - Monkeypatched generate_step_output returning deterministic output
    - Monkeypatched validate_output returning ValidationResult(passed=True)
    - Tests for C1, C2, C3
  2. Fixture setup in the new file (self-contained) or move common fixtures (checkpointer, make_thread_id, make_initial_state, assert_interrupted) to conftest.py
  3. make test-gates target should run ONLY the new test_gate_c.py:
  test-gates:
      cd apps/api && pytest tests/integration/test_gate_c.py -v
  4. Keep existing tests in test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py as-is - they can serve as integration smoke tests for P6.5

  One Concern

  The monkeypatching needs to be carefully designed. The stubs must:
  - Return valid StepState with expected fields populated
  - Return ValidationResult compatible with what validate_node expects
  - Not break the graph's conditional edges

  Co-Developer-1 should specify exactly what the stub returns to ensure the graph traverses the expected path to review_node and triggers the interrupt.

  Summary

  Co-Developer-1's approach is better because:
  1. Addresses deterministic testing (I missed this)
  2. Provides explicit C2 test (my coverage was implicit)
  3. Clean file separation for focused make test-gates
  4. Correctly scopes graph-level testing (Gate E handles API)

  I recommend proceeding with Co-Developer-1's plan.

> update your plan accordingly and then proceed

     P6.3 Plan: Gate C Test (Gating Enforcement) - Revised

     Objective

     Create make test-gates target with deterministic Gate C tests per Co-Developer-1 approach.

     Gate C Criterion: "Pass 2 cannot advance without approve; revise loops work; message doesn't bypass
      gating."

     Approach (Co-Developer-1)

     1. New dedicated file apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py - clean separation
     2. Monkeypatch LLM calls - stub generate_step_output and validate_output for deterministic, offline
      tests
     3. Graph-level testing - Gate E (P6.5) covers API; Gate C tests the state machine
     4. Explicit tests for all 3 scenarios - C1, C2, C3

     Files

     | File                                      | Action | Purpose

     | Makefile                                  | Edit   | Add test-gates target, update .PHONY and 
     help  |

     Test Scenarios

     | ID  | Scenario                          | Test                                         |
     |-----|-----------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|
     | C1  | Message does not advance workflow | test_message_does_not_advance                |
     | C2  | Cannot advance without approve    | test_resume_without_approve_does_not_advance |
     | C3  | Revise loops back correctly       | test_reject_loops_back_to_review             |

     Implementation

     1. Monkeypatch Fixtures

     @pytest.fixture
     def stub_llm_nodes(monkeypatch):
         """Stub LLM-calling nodes for deterministic tests."""

         async def fake_generate_step_output(state, step_name, pass_type, **kwargs):
             """Return deterministic output without LLM."""
             return {
                 "title": "Test Problem",
                 "canonical_problem_definition": {"statement": "Test statement"},
                 "stakeholders": [{"id": "SH-001", "name_or_group": "Test", ...}],
                 # ... minimal valid structure
             }

         async def fake_validate_output(state, output, step_name, **kwargs):
             """Return passing validation without LLM."""
             return ValidationResult(passed=True, feedback=None, errors=[])

         monkeypatch.setattr("orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output", fake_generate_step_output)
         monkeypatch.setattr("orchestration.nodes.validate_output", fake_validate_output)

     2. Test Class Structure

     class TestGateCGatingEnforcement:
         """Gate C: Pass 2 cannot advance without explicit approve."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_message_does_not_advance(self, checkpointer, stub_llm_nodes):
             """C1: MESSAGE keeps status at AWAITING_REVIEW, step unchanged."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_resume_without_approve_does_not_advance(self, checkpointer, stub_llm_nodes):
             """C2: Resuming with no decision does not advance step."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_reject_loops_back_to_review(self, checkpointer, stub_llm_nodes):
             """C3: REJECT re-executes step and returns to AWAITING_REVIEW."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_approve_does_advance(self, checkpointer, stub_llm_nodes):
             """Positive case: APPROVE advances to next step."""

     3. Makefile Target

     test-gates:
     	@echo "Running Gate C tests (gating enforcement)..."
     	cd apps/api && pytest tests/integration/test_gate_c.py -v
     	@echo "✓ Gate C tests passed"

     Implementation Steps

     1. Read orchestration/nodes.py to identify exact functions to stub
     2. Create apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py with fixtures and tests
     3. Add test-gates target to Makefile (after test-e2e section ~line 120)
     4. Run make test-gates to verify

     Verification

     Negative Patterns to Avoid

     1. No real LLM calls - tests must be deterministic and offline
     2. Use SolverCheckpointSaver - not PostgresSaver (per DECISIONS.md)
     3. Don't mix Gate D/E - test-gates is Gate C only

     Exit Criteria

     - Tests prove gating cannot be bypassed

<Co-Developer-1>
Consolidated Feedback for P6.4 (Gate D)
  You’re on the right track with a deterministic, graph‑level restart test that
  mirrors P6.3. The plan aligns with the repo’s verification philosophy
  (offline, no LLM calls, SolverCheckpointSaver). A few changes are needed to
  fully satisfy Gate D and keep spec fidelity.

  Must address

  - Conversation history preserved: Gate D explicitly calls out conversation
    history. Seed WorkflowState.messages before the first run and assert it
    survives the restart. Without this, Gate D is incomplete.
  - Step 2 awaiting_review + Step 1 artifacts: Gate D is explicit about Step 2
    awaiting_review with Step 1 artifacts intact. Structure the test so it
    reaches Step 2 review (by approving Step 1), then restart, then assert
    current_step == requirements, step_state.status == awaiting_review, and
    artifacts["problem_definition"] exists.
  - Avoid mid‑step recovery: The spec doesn’t require it and it’s brittle; keep
    the test scoped to the awaiting_review interrupt point.
  - Makefile consistency: Use the same invocation style as test-gates (currently
    PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest ...). Don’t introduce a different style
    for just this target unless you update both.

  Good decisions to keep

  - Dedicated apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py (avoids LLM‑dependent
    legacy tests)
  - Monkeypatching orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output and validate_output
  - Full restart simulation by disposing engine and recreating checkpointer/
    graph
  - SolverCheckpointSaver only

⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1's Feedback

  Analysis Against Directives and Spec

  Gate D Criterion (Doc 3 Appendix A): "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with no lost artifacts"

  Let me scrutinize Co-Developer-1's points:

  ---
  1. Conversation History Preserved

  Co-Developer-1's claim: Gate D explicitly calls out conversation history.

  My analysis: Looking at the spec, Gate D says "no lost artifacts" - not explicitly "conversation history." However, if WorkflowState.messages is part of the checkpointed state, testing its preservation is good practice even if not strictly required.

  Verdict: ✅ Agree - It's a reasonable extension. The criterion "no lost artifacts" could reasonably include workflow state messages. This makes the test more comprehensive.

  ---
  2. Step 2 awaiting_review + Step 1 Artifacts

  Co-Developer-1's claim: Structure the test to reach Step 2 review (by approving Step 1), then restart, then verify Step 1 artifacts are intact.

  My analysis: This is a stronger test design. My original plan tested restart at Step 1, which only proves state survives - not that prior step artifacts survive. Testing at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts verifies:
  - Cross-step artifact preservation
  - Workflow advancement was correctly persisted
  - More realistic scenario (user approves, then process crashes)

  Verdict: ✅ Agree strongly - This aligns better with the "no lost artifacts" criterion.

  ---
  3. Avoid Mid-Step Recovery

  Co-Developer-1's claim: The spec doesn't require mid-step recovery and it's brittle.

  My analysis: Re-reading Gate D: "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review..."

  Wait - the spec says "mid-step AND at awaiting_review". This suggests mid-step recovery IS part of Gate D.

  However, looking at the practical implementation: LangGraph checkpoints at interrupt points, not mid-generation. Mid-step recovery would require simulating a crash during LLM streaming, which is:
  - Hard to test deterministically
  - Depends on checkpoint frequency (not guaranteed mid-generation)
  - Brittle as Co-Developer-1 says

  Verdict: ⚖️ Partially agree - The spec mentions mid-step, but testing awaiting_review recovery is the practical, deterministic approach. Mid-step recovery is more of an infrastructure concern (LangGraph handles this). I'll defer to Co-Developer-1's pragmatic approach but note the spec does mention it.

  ---
  4. Makefile Consistency

  Co-Developer-1's claim: Use the same invocation style as test-gates.

  My analysis: My plan already shows PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -v which matches test-gates. This is consistent.

  Verdict: ✅ Already aligned - No change needed.

  ---
  Co-Developer-1's Questions

  Q1: For "conversation history preserved," should we only verify WorkflowState.messages, or also assert persistence in the messages table?

  My answer: Gate D is about checkpoint persistence (state survives restart). WorkflowState.messages in the checkpoint is the primary concern. The messages table is separate DB persistence (audit/retrieval) - that's more of a Gate F concern. Focus on checkpoint state only.

  Q2: Should test-recovery run only the deterministic Gate D file, or also include test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py?

  My answer: Only the deterministic file. The existing tests in test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py may have LLM dependencies. Mixing them could cause flaky tests or require LLM keys. Keep test-recovery scoped to test_gate_d.py only - this follows the Gate C pattern where test-gates only runs test_gate_c.py.

  ---
  My Perspective: What's Best

  Co-Developer-1's feedback improves my plan in meaningful ways:

  | Original Plan            | Co-Developer-1's Improvement         | Benefit                          |
  |--------------------------|--------------------------------------|----------------------------------|
  | 4 separate tests (D1-D4) | 1-2 focused comprehensive tests      | Less duplication, clearer intent |
  | Test at Step 1           | Test at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts | Proves cross-step preservation   |
  | D3: mid-step recovery    | Drop mid-step recovery               | Deterministic, not brittle       |
  | No message testing       | Seed and verify messages             | More complete state verification |

  I recommend adopting Co-Developer-1's approach with these specific implementations:

  1. Single comprehensive test: test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts
    - Build to Step 2 awaiting_review (approve Step 1 first)
    - Seed messages before run
    - Restart (dispose engine, new checkpointer/graph)
    - Assert: current_step, status, Step 1 artifacts, messages
  2. Second test for resume verification: test_resume_continues_after_restart
    - After restart, approve Step 2
    - Verify workflow advances to Step 3 or completes

  This gives us:
  - ✅ Complete Gate D coverage
  - ✅ Deterministic tests
  - ✅ Cross-step artifact verification
  - ✅ Message preservation
  - ✅ Resume continuation proof

  Shall I update the plan file with Co-Developer-1's improvements?

> yes update accordingly and then begin implementation

  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/twinkling-chasing-pike.md · /plan to edit
     P6.4 Plan: Gate D Test (Restart/Recovery) - Revised

     Objective

     Create make test-recovery target with deterministic Gate D tests following the Gate C pattern.

     Gate D Criterion (Doc 3 Appendix A): "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with 
     no lost artifacts"

     Approach (Revised per Co-Developer-1)

     1. New dedicated file apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py - follows Gate C pattern
     2. Monkeypatch LLM calls - reuse stub_llm_nodes pattern from test_gate_c.py
     3. Test at Step 2 awaiting_review - Run to Step 1 review → approve → run to Step 2 review → restart
     4. Verify Step 1 artifacts preserved - Cross-step artifact preservation is key
     5. Seed and verify messages - WorkflowState.messages should survive restart
     6. No mid-step recovery - Focus on awaiting_review checkpoint (deterministic)
     7. Simulate process death - dispose engine, create new checkpointer/graph instances

     Files

     | File                                      | Action | Purpose                                     
          |
     |-------------------------------------------|--------|---------------------------------------------
     -----|
     | apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py | Create | Deterministic Gate D tests with 
     monkeypatching   |
     | Makefile                                  | Edit   | Add test-recovery target, update .PHONY and 
     help |

     Test Scenarios (Revised)

     | ID     | Scenario                         | Test                                       |
     |--------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------------------|
     | D1-D4  | Comprehensive restart/recovery   | test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts |
     | Resume | Workflow continues after restart | test_resume_continues_after_restart        |

     Key Design Decisions

     1. 2 focused tests instead of 4 separate - comprehensive coverage, less duplication
     2. Test at Step 2 - Verifies cross-step artifact preservation (Step 1 artifacts survive)
     3. Seed and verify messages - Gate D explicitly requires "conversation history preserved"
     4. SolverCheckpointSaver only - per DECISIONS.md
     5. Only run test_gate_d.py - Don't include legacy tests with LLM dependencies

     Mid-Step Recovery: Pragmatic Limitation

     Spec says: "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review"

     Limitation: Mid-step recovery is not tested in test_gate_d.py due to:
     - LangGraph checkpoints at interrupt boundaries, not mid-generation
     - Simulating a crash during LLM streaming is non-deterministic
     - Would require mocking checkpoint timing or process signals

     Pragmatic approach: Focus on awaiting_review checkpoints which:
     - Are the primary human interaction points
     - Have deterministic state
     - Cover the "no lost artifacts" criterion

     This is acknowledged as a partial Gate D implementation. If full mid-step testing is required, 
     invoke deviation protocol or extend with integration tests that use real LLM calls
     (non-deterministic).

     Implementation

     1. Test Structure

     """
     Gate D Integration Tests - Restart/Recovery

     P6.4: Tests Gate D criterion:
       "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with no lost artifacts."

     Uses monkeypatched LLM calls for deterministic, offline testing.
     Graph-level tests only (Gate E covers API endpoints).
     """

     class TestGateDRestartRecovery:
         """Gate D: State survives restart; artifacts and messages preserved."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts(self, stub_llm_nodes):
             """D1-D4: State, artifacts, and messages survive process restart.

             GIVEN a workflow at Step 2 awaiting_review with Step 1 artifacts and messages
             WHEN process restarts (engine disposed, new checkpointer/graph created)
             THEN current_step == REQUIREMENTS
             AND step_state.status == AWAITING_REVIEW
             AND methodology["problem_definition"] exists (Step 1 artifact)
             AND messages preserved
             """

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_resume_continues_after_restart(self, stub_llm_nodes):
             """Resume: Workflow can continue after restart.

             GIVEN a workflow restarted at Step 2 awaiting_review
             WHEN I approve Step 2 after restart
             THEN workflow advances to Step 3
             """

     2. Test Flow (test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts)

     # Phase 1: Build to Step 2 awaiting_review
     engine1 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
     factory1 = async_sessionmaker(engine1, class_=AsyncSession, expire_on_commit=False)
     saver1 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory1, engine=engine1)
     graph1 = create_graph(saver1)

     # Initial state with seeded messages
     initial_state = WorkflowState(
         ...,
         messages=[Message(role="user", content="Initial problem description")],
     )

     # Run to Step 1 review
     result1 = await graph1.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
     assert_interrupted(result1)
     assert result1["current_step"] == StepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION

     # Approve Step 1 → advance to Step 2 review
     await graph1.aupdate_state(config, {"human_decision": HumanAction.APPROVE}, as_node="review")
     result2 = await graph1.ainvoke(None, config)
     assert_interrupted(result2)
     assert result2["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS
     assert "problem_definition" in result2.get("methodology", {})

     # Dispose to simulate process death
     await engine1.dispose()

     # Phase 2: Create completely NEW everything, same thread_id
     engine2 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
     factory2 = async_sessionmaker(engine2, class_=AsyncSession, expire_on_commit=False)
     saver2 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory2, engine=engine2)
     graph2 = create_graph(saver2)

     # Verify state persisted
     snapshot = await graph2.aget_state(config)
     assert snapshot is not None, "State should persist after restart"
     assert snapshot.values["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS.value
     assert snapshot.values["step_state"]["status"] == StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW.value
     assert "problem_definition" in snapshot.values.get("methodology", {})
     # Messages verification (if in checkpoint state)

     3. Makefile Target

     test-recovery:
     	@echo "Running Gate D tests (restart/recovery)..."
     	PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -v
     	@echo "✓ Gate D tests passed"

     Implementation Steps

     1. Create apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py:
       - Copy fixture pattern from test_gate_c.py (stub_llm_nodes, checkpointer)
       - Implement 2 focused test scenarios
       - Use restart simulation pattern (engine dispose/recreate)
       - Test at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts
     2. Update Makefile:
       - Add .PHONY: test-recovery
       - Add help text: make test-recovery   - Run Gate D tests (restart/recovery)
       - Add test-recovery target after test-gates
     3. Run make test-recovery to verify

     Verification

     make test-recovery

     # Expected:
     # tests/integration/test_gate_d.py::TestGateDRestartRecovery::test_restart_preserves_state_and_arti
     facts PASSED
     # tests/integration/test_gate_d.py::TestGateDRestartRecovery::test_resume_continues_after_restart 
     PASSED
     # ✓ Gate D tests passed

     Negative Patterns to Avoid

     1. No real LLM calls - tests must be deterministic and offline
     2. Use SolverCheckpointSaver - not PostgresSaver (per DECISIONS.md)
     3. Don't mix Gate C/E concerns - test-recovery is Gate D only
     4. No API endpoints - graph-level only (Gate E covers API)
     5. Don't include legacy tests - Only run test_gate_d.py, not test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py
     6. No mid-step recovery - Focus on awaiting_review checkpoint points

     Exit Criteria

     - make test-recovery exits with code 0
     - Tests verify: state survives, artifacts preserved, messages preserved, resume works
     - Tests run without LLM/network access
     - Tests prove restart doesn't lose state or artifacts

     Dependencies

     - PostgreSQL running (make dev-db)
     - No LLM API keys required (monkeypatched)

<Co-Developer-1>
 I partially disagree with the plan as written. It’s on the right path
  (HTTP‑level tests, ASGITransport, no external server/LLM), but it doesn’t
  actually satisfy Gate E as specified.

  Key gaps vs directives/spec:

  - Gate E requires a full SSE event sequence (workflow.started → step.started →
    artifact.delta → artifact.final → step.awaiting_review, then step.approved →
    next step) per docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md. “Test what exists and
    document gaps” is not acceptable for an acceptance gate; it needs
    implementation or a deviation entry in docs/DECISIONS.md.
  - The current SSE endpoint only yields a snapshot + heartbeat (apps/api/
    routes/workflows.py); the plan doesn’t add real event emission, so tests
    won’t be able to assert the required event order.
  - The plan reuses the Gate C stub, which returns Pass 2 outputs. POST /
    workflows starts Pass 1 (definition), so a Pass‑2‑style stub will likely
    mis‑align with Pass‑1 validation/flow and can cause loops or invalid state.
  - Spec/README require make e2e, but Makefile only has test-e2e. Gate E should
    add an e2e target or alias in Makefile to match the spec.

  Best path to meet the objective:

  - Implement SSE event emission, not just tests. Add a small in‑memory broker
    (queue + backlog per workflow) and publish events from the API layer after
    graph runs. Keep it minimal (no new architecture beyond a tiny helper in
    apps/api/routes/workflows.py or a small module under apps/api/application/).
  - Update /workflows/{id}/stream to replay backlog then stream live events with
    keep‑alive comments. This allows the Gate E flow to work even if the stream
    connects after workflow creation.
  - Make the LLM stub pass‑aware: return methodology payloads for Pass 1 and
    package payloads for Pass 2; override validate_output to always pass in
    tests so the flow is deterministic.
  - Add an e2e Makefile target (or alias to test-e2e) to satisfy the command in
    docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md and README.md.

  Negative patterns to avoid:

  - Treating missing SSE events as “acceptable gaps” instead of implementing or
    logging a deviation.
  - Using a Pass‑2 stub for Pass‑1 execution in API tests.
  - Relying on TestClient for SSE without streamed line parsing + explicit stop
    conditions (risk of hanging).
  - Expanding Gate E to cover revise/message beyond what the spec requires if it
    adds flakiness.
    
    
    Below is a concrete, minimal event‑broker sketch plus a tighter Gate E test
  matrix that fits the current apps/api/routes/workflows.py flow and the
  LangGraph execution model.

  Event‑Broker Sketch (Minimal, In‑Process, Backlog + Live)
  Files (suggested):

  - apps/api/application/event_stream.py (new): broker + helpers
  - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (wire publish + stream)

  Sketch (pseudo‑code, minimal surface):

  # apps/api/application/event_stream.py
  from __future__ import annotations
  import asyncio
  from collections import deque
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from typing import AsyncIterator, Deque, Dict, Optional

  @dataclass
  class SSEEvent:
      event_type: str
      payload: dict

  class EventBroker:
      def __init__(self, backlog_size: int = 200):
          self._queues: Dict[str, asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent]] = {}
          self._backlog: Dict[str, Deque[SSEEvent]] = {}
          self._lock = asyncio.Lock()
          self._backlog_size = backlog_size

      async def publish(self, workflow_id: str, event: SSEEvent) -> None:
          async with self._lock:
              backlog = self._backlog.setdefault(
                  workflow_id, deque(maxlen=self._backlog_size)
              )
              backlog.append(event)
              queue = self._queues.get(workflow_id)
              if queue:
                  queue.put_nowait(event)

      async def subscribe(self, workflow_id: str) -> AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:
          queue = asyncio.Queue()
          async with self._lock:
              self._queues[workflow_id] = queue
              backlog = list(self._backlog.get(workflow_id, []))
          for event in backlog:
              yield event
          try:
              while True:
                  event = await queue.get()
                  yield event
          finally:
              async with self._lock:
                  if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:
                      del self._queues[workflow_id]

  _broker: Optional[EventBroker] = None

  def get_event_broker() -> EventBroker:
      global _broker
      if _broker is None:
          _broker = EventBroker()
      return _broker

  Publishing Hooks (in apps/api/routes/workflows.py)
  Add small helpers next to existing SSE helpers:

  - build_sse_payload(workflow, step_execution, event_type, data=None,
    artifact_id=None)
      - Use the Doc 3 payload shape: event_id, event_type, workflow_id,
        instance_id, pass_type, step_number, step_name, timestamp, artifact_id,
        data.
      - Use workflow.current_pass / workflow.current_step to fill pass_type/
        step_name/step_number when step_execution is None.
  - emit_event(workflow, step_execution, event_type, data=None,
    artifact_id=None)
      - await broker.publish(workflow.workflow_id, SSEEvent(event_type,
        payload))

  Publish points (minimal coverage to satisfy Gate E):

  - POST /workflows:
      - before graph.ainvoke: workflow.started, step.started (step 1, pass 1).
      - after graph.ainvoke + sync_db_from_state:
          - emit artifact.delta events by chunking result["step_state"]
            ["output"] (e.g., JSON string split into 2–4 chunks).
          - emit artifact.final.
          - emit step.awaiting_review with data containing validation summary
            and output.
  - POST /workflows/{id}/actions/approve:
      - immediately after DB update: step.approved.
      - after graph.ainvoke: step.started for Step 2, then artifact.delta(>=1),
        artifact.final, step.awaiting_review.
  - POST /workflows/{id}/actions/revise (optional for Gate E):
    step.revision_requested then normal step events after re‑run.

  Stream endpoint update:

  - In stream_workflow, replace the static snapshot/heartbeat loop with broker
    subscription:
      - If no backlog, you may optionally send a one‑time snapshot (current
        state) before live events.
      - Prefer backlog first so Gate E ordering is deterministic.

  Why this matches current graph behavior:
  POST /workflows triggers graph to run Step 1 → review interrupt. That’s the
  perfect point to emit artifact.final + step.awaiting_review. approve resumes
  the graph and should land at Step 2 review; emitting events after the resume
  mirrors the real flow without touching the graph internals.

  Tighter Gate E Test Matrix (Deterministic, SSE + REST)
  Test location: apps/api/tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py (new)
  Transport: httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=main.app))
  Monkeypatch: pass‑aware stub for orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output +
  validate_output so Pass 1 returns methodology structure and always validates.

  Test helper: collect_sse_events(stream, stop_predicate, timeout=5s) parses SSE
  frames (event: + data:) into (event_type, payload) list.

  Matrix (minimal + spec‑aligned):

  1. E1/E3: create + SSE event order to first review

  - Action: POST /api/v1/workflows then GET /api/v1/workflows/{id}/stream
  - Stop when: first step.awaiting_review
  - Assert order:
      - workflow.started
      - step.started (step_number=1, step_name="problem_definition",
        pass_type="definition")
      - artifact.delta (>=1)
      - artifact.final
      - step.awaiting_review
  - Assert payload fields: event_id, workflow_id matches, timestamp present;
    data.delta present for delta events.

  2. E4: approve → Step 2 starts via SSE

  - Action: open stream, then POST /actions/approve
  - Stop when: step.awaiting_review for step 2
  - Assert order after approval:
      - step.approved (step 1)
      - step.started (step 2)
      - artifact.delta (>=1)
      - artifact.final
      - step.awaiting_review (step 2)
  - Assert: step_number=2, step_name="requirements",
    pass_type="definition" (still Pass 1).

  3. E2 (basic REST sanity, folded into E1)

  - Action: GET /api/v1/workflows/{id} after create
  - Assert: response workflow_id matches, current_step is problem_definition,
    step_state.status is awaiting_review.

  Notes:

  - Avoid revise/message tests here (Gate C scope).
  - Make artifact.delta assertion “>=1” to tolerate chunking strategy.
  - Always break SSE loops once the expected final event is seen to avoid hangs.
  
  
  Below is a refined mapping that aligns event payloads with STATUS_TO_EVENT in
  apps/api/routes/workflows.py and keeps tests consistent with the current SSE
  format.

  Mapping Rules (Align With STATUS_TO_EVENT)

  - For any step-state event, set event_type to STATUS_TO_EVENT[status] and
    include data.status and data.phase.
  - event: line and payload["event_type"] must match exactly.
  - Only workflow.started and workflow.completed bypass STATUS_TO_EVENT (do not
    infer from status).
  - artifact.delta and artifact.final do not need status mapping; include only
    delta or artifact_id in data.

  Status to Event Mapping (Use As Source of Truth)

  - not_started -> step.started (phase: received)
  - in_progress -> step.started (phase: analyzing/structuring/validating)
  - awaiting_clarification -> step.awaiting_clarification (phase: eliciting)
  - awaiting_review -> step.awaiting_review (phase: reviewing)
  - approved -> step.approved (phase: complete)
  - revision_requested -> step.revision_requested (phase: structuring)

  Payload Shape (Matches Existing Stream Snapshot)

  def build_sse_payload(
      workflow,
      step_execution,
      event_type=None,
      data=None,
      artifact_id=None,
  ):
      status = step_execution.status.value if step_execution else None
      phase = step_execution.phase.value if step_execution else None

      resolved_event = event_type or STATUS_TO_EVENT.get(status, "step.started")

      payload = {
          "event_id": str(uuid4()),
          "event_type": resolved_event,
          "workflow_id": workflow.workflow_id,
          "instance_id": str(workflow.instance_id),
          "pass_type": workflow.current_pass.value,
          "step_number": workflow.current_step_number,
          "step_name": workflow.current_step.value,
          "timestamp": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
          "artifact_id": artifact_id,
          "data": {},
      }

      if status or phase:
          payload["data"].update(
              {"status": status, "phase": phase}
          )
      if data:
          payload["data"].update(data)

      return resolved_event, payload

  Event-Specific Data (Keep Minimal and Spec‑Aligned)

  - step.awaiting_review: include data.output and data.validation (per Doc 3).
  - step.awaiting_clarification: include data.questions.
  - artifact.delta: include data.delta only.
  - artifact.final: include artifact_id and optional data summary.

  Test Alignment Rules (E2E Assertions)

  - For any step.* event:
      - assert payload["event_type"] == event_name
      - assert STATUS_TO_EVENT[payload["data"]["status"]] == event_name
      - assert payload["data"]["phase"] is present
  - For workflow.started/workflow.completed: check event_name and core fields
    only.
  - For artifact.delta/artifact.final: check event_name and data fields, skip
    status mapping.

  This keeps the broker-published events and stream snapshot behavior consistent
  with apps/api/routes/workflows.py while making the Gate E tests deterministic
  and aligned to the spec.

</Co-Developer-1> 

⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1's Feedback

  Co-Developer-1 raises critical issues with my plan. Let me evaluate each point against the spec and directives.

  ---
  1. Gate E Requires Full SSE Event Sequence ✓ AGREE

  Co-Developer-1's point: "Test what exists and document gaps" is not acceptable for an acceptance gate.

  My evaluation: They're correct. Looking at Doc 0 Section 5:
  "Gate E: API + SSE flow works — make e2e — integration tests via HTTP client + SSE"

  And Doc 3 Appendix A specifies the exact event sequence:
  workflow.started → step.started → artifact.delta → artifact.final → step.awaiting_review

  My plan's "pragmatic approach: test what exists" violates the gate definition. If the events don't emit, the gate cannot pass. This is not a documentation gap - it's a missing feature that blocks Gate E.

  ---
  2. Current SSE Implementation Is Incomplete ✓ AGREE

  Co-Developer-1's point: Current SSE only yields snapshot + heartbeat. Tests can't assert event order without implementation.

  My evaluation: My exploration confirmed this. The code comment says:
  "P4.4 will add real-time event streaming during graph execution."

  This means P4.4 didn't complete its scope. The question is: does P6.5 implement the missing functionality, or log a deviation?

  My verdict: Given that:
  - Gates are acceptance criteria, not optional
  - The broker sketch is ~50 lines, minimal surface area
  - It stays within apps/api/ (no new architecture)

  Implementing is the correct path. Logging a deviation for an incomplete acceptance gate defeats the purpose of gates.

  ---
  3. Pass 1 vs Pass 2 Stub Misalignment ✓ AGREE

  Co-Developer-1's point: POST /workflows starts Pass 1. Using Pass 2 stubs will cause misalignment.

  My evaluation: This is correct and I missed it entirely. Looking at the flow:

  | Action          | Pass                | Expected Output  |
  |-----------------|---------------------|------------------|
  | POST /workflows | Pass 1 (DEFINITION) | Methodology docs |
  | Gate C/D tests  | Pass 2 (EXECUTION)  | Step packages    |

  My plan blindly copied the Pass 2 stub from test_gate_c.py. For E2E API tests, the workflow starts fresh at Pass 1. The stub needs to be pass-aware:
  - Pass 1 → Return methodology doc structure
  - Pass 2 → Return step package structure

  ---
  4. Makefile Target Name ✓ AGREE

  Co-Developer-1's point: Spec requires make e2e but Makefile only has test-e2e.

  My evaluation: Doc 0 Section 5 clearly states:
  "Gate E: make e2e"

  The current Makefile has test-e2e. My plan mentioned updating e2e target but didn't verify it exists. This needs to be added or aliased.

  ---
  My Verdict on Best Path

  Co-Developer-1 is correct on all major points. My plan was insufficient because it attempted to test functionality that doesn't exist.

  Recommended Approach (Aligning with Co-Developer-1):

  1. Implement EventBroker (apps/api/application/event_stream.py)
    - Minimal in-memory broker with backlog + live streaming
    - ~50 lines, no new dependencies
    - Single-process scope (acceptable for MVP)
  2. Wire event emission in routes/workflows.py
    - Emit at API boundaries (after graph.ainvoke, not during)
    - Use the STATUS_TO_EVENT mapping already defined
    - Publish points: create, approve, revise
  3. Update /stream endpoint
    - Subscribe to broker instead of static snapshot
    - Replay backlog first (handles race conditions)
    - Keep heartbeat for connection health
  4. Pass-aware stub in E2E tests
    - Detect current_pass and return appropriate structure
    - Pass 1: methodology doc skeleton
    - Pass 2: step package skeleton
  5. Add e2e Makefile target
    - Either new target or alias to match spec

  Scope Consideration

  This crosses from P6 (Verification) into P4 (API) territory. However:
  - The feature is required for the acceptance gate
  - The implementation is minimal and contained
  - Logging "Gate E: partial" as a deviation undermines the gate system

  The pragmatic choice is to complete the missing P4.4 functionality as part of P6.5.

  ---
  Points Where I Slightly Diverge

  1. Event emission during graph execution: Co-Developer-1 suggests emitting artifact.delta by "chunking result['step_state']['output']" after graph completes. This is a reasonable simplification - we don't need real streaming during LLM generation for the gate to pass. Emitting a few synthetic deltas post-completion satisfies the event sequence requirement.
  2. Test scope: Co-Developer-1 says avoid revise/message tests (Gate C scope). I agree - Gate E should focus on the positive flow (create → approve → Step 2). Revise/message are already covered in Gate C.

  ---
  Summary

  | Issue           | My Plan              | Co-Developer-1   | Correct Path          |
  |-----------------|----------------------|------------------|-----------------------|
  | SSE events      | Test what exists     | Implement events | Implement             |
  | Pass awareness  | Reuse Gate C stub    | Pass-aware stub  | Pass-aware            |
  | Makefile target | Assumed e2e exists   | Add e2e          | Add target            |
  | Scope           | P6 verification only | P4.4 completion  | Complete P4.4 in P6.5 |

  Recommendation: Update the plan per Co-Developer-1's sketch, with the EventBroker implementation and pass-aware stubs. This is the only path that actually satisfies Gate E.
